 Confidenza (NOIA remix)
 Review: Marco Battistini keeps himself in the shadows as a bureaucratic curator of a secret archive. 'Il Segreto' opens a trilogy about an affair on the early-80s Italian Riviera that never came to light. The sound is Italo disco with all of the usual varnish scraped off: gritty drums, saturated synths, bass that throbs, all coalescing into an unashamedly catchy tune. Daft Punk's machine cool and Righeira's wry grin are both in there. The N.O.I.A. remix is a nice touch, since the group formed in Cervia in 1978 as they attempted to echo Kraftwerk's meticulous electronic funk. … Read more

 HypnoFreak (8:19)

 All Night Long (7:43)
 Review: Relaunched turn-of-the-Millennium West Coast house imprint Pacific Coast Recordings continues to serve up must-check reissues. This two-tracker first appeared as a promo-only missive on the label's long dormant Pacific Traxx offshoot and comes from peripheral crew member (and regular JT Donaldson collaborator) Chris Penny AKA CPEN. 'Hypno Freak' paddles in the funkier end of the California deep house spectrum, with crisp funk guitar licks, tight electric piano stabs and gritty spoken word vocals rising above a weighty bassline and heavy, locked-in beats. In contrast, 'All Night Long' is a more traditional chunk of lightly tech-tinged peak-time San Francisco deep house with just the right amount of frazzled late-night funk. … Read more

 Can't Help Me
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1169073-01-02-02.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1169073-01-02-02.mp3] 
 Pressure
 Review: Run between Barcelona and Berlin by Moon&Mann aka Da Silva and DJ Katah, MM Discos has been serving up high-grade treats since the tail end of 2015. Here they follow-up successful recent missives from Nic Jalusi and Daichi with a Balearic-minded - but undoubtedly dancefloor-ready - five-tracker courtesy of Spencer Terry's Desert Island Disco collective. They first doff a cap to the mid-tempo warmth, sunshine and synthesiser colours of 1980s South African bubblegum on the gorgeous 'Ifuna', before breaking up the beats a little on the dub disco-goes-kwaito excellence of 'Ikuna'. 'Iyo' fuses the low-end pulse of Italo-disco with summery South African synth-pop, 'Can't Help Me' is a dreamy and loved-up Balearic house masterpiece, and 'Pressure' is an authentic-sounding chunk of late-80s kwaito brilliance. A genuine must-check EP. … Read more

 You Can't Change A Man (4:14)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1148429-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1148429-01-02-01.mp3] 
 Take Life Easy (4:02)
 Review: Lagos nightlife wisdom distilled in a groove, courtesy of white label suppliers Dynamite Cuts. Christy Essien-Igbokwe cut 'Take Life Easy' and 'You Can't Change A Man' for her fourth album One Understanding, coming in 1979 after a much-publicised move from teenage TV regular to defining Nigerian voice. Agile across Lagos studios, hers is a ticking mix of highlife, soul and Afrofunk; the A's a gently instructive song, a parental refrain looping through the lyrics as rhythm and message merge. The B is much sprightlier, and comes with a forthright subject matter: women and men simply can't force change on each other. Away from the studio, Essien-Igbokwe channelled her zest for life and music into advocacy, becoming the first female president of the Performing Musicians Association of Nigeria and supporting women's and children's rights through public work. … Read more

 Family Tree (OST Super Disco Break Beat) (3:06)
 Review: Whether you're into disco, funk, soul, hip-hop or a mix of all four, you should be familiar with Family Tree's self-titled debut single featuring future boogie hero Sharon Brown. It has long been considered a classic by DJs of all these persuasions, with its breaks being amongst those spun during the NYC block parties that spurned hip-hop culture. This remastered Acid Jazz reissue brings together the three most familiar versions of the cut. The real killer cut is the A-side 1976 disco version, which extends and toughens up the cut while retaining all of the vocals and instrumentation. Over on the flip, the band's original shorter seven version is joined by Dean Rudland, Frank Tope and Lord Brixton's 2002 'Super Disco Break Beat' take, a killer drum dub style edit with quick-mix style flashes of the original song. … Read more

 Not So Disco Queen (B Love remix)
 Review: On its third missive of 2026, Elon Dust's vinyl only Sex Tapes From Mars imprint has turned to Andy Kas, a rising star of the European underground best known for his previous outings on Proper Grub and Mindhelmet. There's a pleasing cheeriness about opener 'Chordster', a squelchy, melody-rich and analogue-driven fusion of deep house and 80s-influenced nu-disco, while 'Music Takes Me' is a thickset throb-job topped off with sampled diva vocals. Side B is all about 'Not So Disco Queen', with Kas's original mix - a driving chunk of synth-heavy, Italo-disco-influenced early house hedonism - coming accompanied by a deeper electro-meets-breakbeat house reinterpretation by B-Love. … Read more

 Dirty Talk (instrumental edit) (5:23)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186185-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1186185-01-02-01.mp3] 
 Cannonball (instrumental edit) (5:23)
 Review: Two 1980s instrumentals get sharpened to fit another must-cop 7" from the one and only edit king, Mr. K. The American maestro starts with Klein & MBO's 'Dirty Talk,' the 1982 Italo jewel whose locked bassline and 808 groove pointed toward pop and house, and which New Order reportedly borrowed from a year later for their evergreen 'Blue Monday.' He trims it to the essentials and keeps the synth pulse front and centre. The flip takes on Supertramp's 'Cannonball,' a 1985 club curveball built around a single key that here gets stripped back to its pounding keys and big brass swells. It's the first time the instrumental has appeared on 7" + this one's limited, so don't snooze. … Read more

 Free Your Mind (3:50)

 Your Fantasy (4:00)
 Review: 'Free Your Mind' is a rare and sought-after funk single from 1979, originally released on the obscure Gem City label. Though this debut was their only proper release, Record Player were a humungous supergroup from Dayton, Ohio, with over fifteen members exiting and entering their collective revolving door over the course of their active period. It's clear that the single's first reissue back in 2006 (via Soul Cal) wasn't sufficient for the most avid diggers out there, so now Fraternity/Und Brother team up again for round two, again backing up the song with the sultry wish-fulfiller 'Your Fantasy'. … Read more

 Wanna B (6:34)
 Review: It's time to stand to attention, as Sgt Slick once more bellows out orders to the dancefloor via four more party-starting 'recuts'. The veteran Aussie producer kicks off volume eight in his ongoing series via 'Shampagne', a lightly straightened and tooled-up house style rearrangement of a warming, much-loved boogie classic, before opting for a bigger, bolder, turn of the 90s-esque house fix of what sounds like a cover of disco favourite 'Going Back To My Roots'. B-side 'Opener' is a joyous tweak of a horn-heavy disco-funk workout from one of San Francisco disco's most beloved icons, while 'Wanna B' enters 'end of night territory' via a superb tweak of a hybrid disco/electrofunk mega-hit. … Read more

 The Sunburst Band - "Reach For My Love" (feat Tiffany T'zelle - Special 45 version) (4:29)
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1132181-01-02-01.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1132181-01-02-01.mp3] 
 Queen B Unlimited - - "27 Sided" (Special 45 version) (4:34)
 Review: We've got two brand spanking new exclusives on the latest Z Records 7-inch record. First up is 'Reach For It' from The Sunburst Band with its upliftingAMtume/Lucas feel, glorious vocals from New Jersey singer Tiffany T'zelle (fans will know her from Dave Lee's 'Produced With Love II' album) and all the usual star players of The Sunburst Band. Then, making her debut on ZR is Queen B Unlimited with '27 Sided', an ultra-smooth dose of modern disco-soul. It's an expertly produced love song about us all being different shapes, trying to find our match. We suggest turning the lights down low for this one. … Read more

 Love Lesson (5:16)
 Review: Boogie obscurity alert! If you've not heard of Twin Image, you're not alone; the female sibling duo recorded just one album for Capitol Records way back in 1984, with producer Sigidi at the helm, and it wasn't a commercial success - despite the set featuring a handful of real gems. It's from that set, Mirror, that these two cuts are taken. A-side 'Kiss & Make It Better', a cheery, funky and delightfully synth-heavy affair that reminded us of the kind of electrofunk-meets-soul excellence released by Los Angeles label Solar System at the time, was the pair's club banger and still has the power to get dancefloors moving. 'Love Lesson', meanwhile, is a slower and more sensual jam that nevertheless is packed to the rafters with colourful synth sounds, squelchy bass and soulful vocals. … Read more

 Time (45 version) (4:09)
 Review: Throughout the 1980s, Joey Jefferson's independent California Gold label offered up synth-funk, boogie, rap and electro. Not many of the releases were commercially successful, despite their clear quality, meaning that few bar the most dedicated collectors know of their existence. Happily, this reissue boasts one of the imprint's most potent and sought-after releases, Zenith's sole release from 1984. 'Groove Time USA' - which is showcased in both extended and edited forms - is a fine chunk of synth-powered boogie soul rich in elastic slap-bass, heady group vocals, clipped guitar licks and snaking sax solos. We also get original B-side 'Time', a heartfelt chunk of slow-motion synth-soul tailor-made for tender moments. … Read more

 Unfinished Rhapsodie (3:21)
 Review: Back in 1987, following the break-up of his phenomenally successful Euro-synth-pop outfit Modern Talking, German singer/producer Dieter Bohlen launched a new project: Euro-dance outfit Blue System. While the project was not particularly successful in Britain and America, it was a runaway success in his native Germany (and, for some reason, Russia). Hello America, the fluid collective's seventh studio album, first hit stores in spring 1992. It's an interesting curio: a high-camp affair that blends Hi-NRG synth-pop and Euro-dance grooves with string-laden cuts that pay tribute to musical theatre, New Jack Swing, slow-motion ballads, flamenco and the vibrant colours of early 90s Italo-house. … Read more

 (Funny) Bone
 Review: First released in 1978 at the height of their fame, C'est Chic is undoubtedly the single-greatest full-length from disco's greatest band, Nile Rodgers and Bernard Edwards NYC ensemble Chic. The finest distillation of their sound - think crispy guitar licks, soulful vocals, rubbery bass, tight beats and sweeping strings - it not only contains a string of must-have anthems (the instantly recognisable 'Chic Cheer', 'Le Freak' and 'I Want Your Love'), but also untold other highlights - not least the sumptuous, jazz-flecked downtempo beauty of 'Savoir Faire', the sweet 'Sometimes You Win' and the often re-edited excellence of '(Funny) Bone'. If you don't yet own a copy, you need this reissue in your life. … Read more

 Stand Up (6:27)
 Review: You wait ages for a Da Lukas album and then two come along at once. Amazingly, it took the popular Italian producer (real name Salvo De Luca) over a quarter of a century to eventually deliver his debut album, Rhythm Heritage; six months later, he's back with LP number two, the more expansive Get Back. In keeping with the Groove Culture label's signature sound, it sees De Luca deliver sample-rich cuts that giddily blur the boundaries between funk, soul, disco, boogie and peak-time ready old-school house. There's plenty to enjoy across the set, from the Clavinet-sporting heavy funk-house of 'Drop The Funk', the soaring revivalist disco bounce of 'Get Back', the swirling, string-laden disco-house funkiness of 'Sicily Theme', the Latin disco-funk exuberance of 'Viva' and the soulful house rush of 'Stand Up'. … Read more

 Duran Duran, Nile Rogers - "Free To Love" (Across The Galaxy) (5:00)
 Review: Somewhere in the back of Nick Rhodes' brain, apparently beside the vodka, sat a Duran Duran song deemed far too sunny for Danse Macabre. Birmingham's old futurists have finally thawed it out. 'Free to Love' starts with a Nile Rodgers riff and behaves accordingly, all champagne-bubble guitar and a chorus with its shirt sleeves rolled up. Rhodes had shelved it for being too cheerful, and in hindsight, that was probably the selling point. There's something pleasingly unembarrassed about a band this seasoned making pure uplift with no conceptual furniture attached. … Read more

 Review: Second time around for Golf Trip, a project that draws together such disparate artists as Norwegian producer Olefunken, Swiss vocalist Sreya and British harpist Rachel Kitchlew. The fluid collective's second full-length (the first landed back in 2022), it's reportedly "built around the idea of escape". It's loosely rooted in Balearica and dreamy, jazz-funk-flecked indie-pop, with a warming and hard-to-pigeonhole sound that's rich in slow-motion rhythms, heady basslines, dream-pop-adjacent guitars, soft-rock sax solos and warming, tactile synth chords. It sounds like the kind of thing designed to be listened to late at night and early in the morning, with opaque colours, deliciously huggable grooves and dewy-eyed vocals. … Read more

 Music That You Can Dance To (demo version 6)
 Review: A record executive asked Sparks for Music That You Can Dance To, and the Los Angeles brothers duly obliged, albeit with a raised eyebrow you could probably see from space. Reissued for its 40th anniversary, their 1986 album gives Russell Mael's vocal gymnastics a gloriously overvarnished synthpop setting. 'Fingertips' gets wonderfully silly with slap bass and falsetto, then 'Shopping Mall Of Love' lets Ron turn retail therapy into faintly unhinged electro theatre. The production occasionally threatens to swallow the furniture but the songs have sharp enough teeth to survive it. … Read more

 Change (7:57)
 Review: Los Angeles had already watched the Mael brothers treat pop like a dressing-up box by 1986 and Music That You Can Dance To catches Sparks in one of their glossiest costumes. 'Change' is the keeper, Russell Mael worrying at mortality over a six-minute arrangement that keeps mutating beneath him, slyly funny and faintly unnerving. 'Modesty Blaise' has more bite. 'Rosebud', meanwhile, lets Ron Mael poke around in oddball Americana again, and even 'Shopping Mall of Love' gets mileage from its deliberately plasticky little tune. The weirdness keeps seeping through the lacquer. … Read more

 Worakls - "Adagio For Square"
 Review: 50 years ago, the former home and gardens of a Spanish aristocrat was transformed into the open-air club that would do more than any other to found the Balearic music movement, and later introduce house to Ibiza: Amnesia. To mark the occasion, the venue has offered up this colossal, ten-disc box set featuring some of the umpteen now anthems that made dancers move over the years. There's a splash of disco (Chic, Donna Summer), synth-powered 80s dancefloor hits (Anne Clark, New Order, Propaganda), early Balearic favourites(Chris Rea, Antenna, George Kranz, Electra) and vintage house gems (Joe Smooth, Sueno Latino), as well as trance (Paul Van Dyke, Binary Finary, Robert Miles, Jam and Spoon), minimal (Audion, Michel Cleis), 21st century EDM (Calvin Harris) and all-time anthems aplenty (Jaydee, Laurent Garnier, Hardfloor remixing Mary Kante). A fine celebration packed with some of dance music's greatest moments from the last half a century. … Read more

 Neriman - "Sever Gibi"
 [link: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1177271-01-02-05.mp3] [audio: https://www.juno.co.uk/MP3/SF1177271-01-02-05.mp3] 
 Tele Tempo - "Havada Bulut Yok"
 Review: Grup Ses digs deep into Turkey's overlooked pop history with this fascinating collection of 80s oddities, and they all trace how new studio technology reshaped the country's musical landscape during that transformative decade. In the wake of the 1980 military coup, drum machines, synthesisers and samplers began turning up in recording studios and opening up new possibilities for local artists. From glossy synth-pop inspired by international trends to offbeat fusions of electronic production, Arabesque and Anatolian pop, this magnificently varied and colourful compilation captures a scene in transition and bursting with new ideas and fresh fusions. It's deep dive into an unpredictable side of Turkish music where tradition meets the machines. … Read more

 Heart Break - "Word Problem" (4:52)
 Review: Legendary first-wave Italo-disco artist Fred Ventura is the man at the helm of this collection of 'modern disco', an eight-track set of previously unheard exclusives that shows the enduring legacy of the style that made him a star. A quick glance at the list of artists involved should be enough to get the juices flowing, with all involved being expert at blurring the boundaries between the original synth-pop influenced sounds of classic Italo and harder, rougher and more intense contemporary interpretations. For proof, check the fine contributions from Tyrell Corporation v Italoconnection, Sfera Celeste Project, Also Playable Mono and DJ TinTin & Go. … Read more

 Jestofunk - "Say It Again" (Micky More & Andy Tee remix edit)
 Review: A double header of edited remixes here, as Groove Culture's ongoing 7" series continues to offer up must-have disco missives. On side A, French great Dimitri From Paris gets his mitts on Double Dee's 1990 Italo-house classic, 'Found Love', re-imaging it as a chunk of synth-splashed nu-disco warmth peppered with references to the original (Dany's vocals, the cut's famous piano riff and blasts of UK house breakbeats included).  X-plor16 (Resonants)
 Review: On his ongoing Explorations series for home city label Permanent Vacation, sometime Dekmantel contributor Sam Goku has generally defied expectations, offering up interesting and left-of-centre dancefloor jams that regularly draw on a variety of influences and inspirations. The fourth volume in the series fits this mould, with the Munich-based artist first offering a dusty, dub techno head’s take on picturesque Japanese jazz-house (‘X-Ploration 13 (Nothing Blue)’, before opting for a more driving, sun-soaked, sub-heavy and percussively sweaty deep house sound on the similarly beautiful ‘X-Ploration 14 (Gardens)’. Over on side B, ‘X-Ploration 15 (Second Thoughts)’ pairs shaker-soaked minimal house grooves with waves of stirring melodic motifs, while ‘X-Ploration 16 (Resonants)’ is a mind-mangling, head-stretching trip tailor-made for more psychedelic dancefloors. … Read more

 Untamed Dub
 Review: Lauded French reggae label Jamwax has decided to take a deep dive into the catalogue of British lovers rock icon Peter Huningdale (AKA Mr Honeyvibes), returning with a sought-rarity from 1986. Recorded in his home city of London, ‘Untamed Love’ is not a lovers rock gem, but rather a fusion of post-boogie 80s soul and what would later become street soul – all elastic slap bass, bright and colourful synths, shuffling electro beats and Huningdale’s deliciously smooth and emotive lead vocals. As it did back in ’86, it comes backed by the similarly excellent ‘Untamed Dub’, a rhythm-focused and club-ready interpretation that naturally contains plenty of delay-style effects, previously buried solos and some break-dance-ready beat sections. … Read more

 Review: With years of experience behind him, Sgt Slick is the undisputed leader of Australia's rapidly expanding re-edit battalion. He communicates with his troops via the ongoing Slick Cuts series, his ongoing contribution to global re-edit culture. Volume nine sees him lead the charge towards peak-time dancefloors, first via the string-laden, hands-aloft disco-meets-house excellence of R&B sampling opener 'Night Queen'. He doffs a cap to the pulsating, arpeggio-driven throb of Italo-disco and Giorgio Moroder on 'Video Life', before combining sweet Sade vocals with a gorgeously sunny and musically detailed disco-house groove on 'Love Hang'. Finally, he gets us to stand to attention once more on dreamy, saucer-eyed closer 'Trouble', a Balearic disco style rub of what sounds like a late 80s AOR pop gem. … Read more

 Super People (instrumental)
 Review: Second time around for ‘Super People’, a sought-after seven-inch from Californian multi-instrumentalist and producer Sunlightsquare (real name Claudio Passvanti) which originally appeared in stores in late 2015. Featuring trumpet solos by Quentin Collins and heady lead vocals from Rene Alvarez and Tasita D’Mour, it’s a warming and rolling chunk of lightly synth-splashed, Rhodes-rich Latin jazz-funk/disco fusion that remains one of the single greatest cuts in the artist’s expansive catalogue. This time round, it comes backed with the previously unreleased instrumental take rather than his cover of ‘Papa is a Rolling Stone’. That’s no bad thing, as Passvanti’s arrangement and playing are superb. … Read more

 Our Day's Been Coming (feat Kevin "Slow Jammin" James & Moniquea)
 Review: While Matthew Hudgins’ earliest outings as XL Middleton were G-funk affairs (he was adept at crafting synth-splashed beats for rappers to rhyme over), he’s spent much of the latest 16 years at the forefront of the revivalist synth-funk scene. Private Press, his first album in two years, sees him in that made, delivering colourful, squelchy, tactile and infectious workouts that not only tap into his usual revivalist West Coast boogie sound, but also the insatiable sexiness of G-funk and the intergalactic excellence of original Detroit P-funk. As you’d expect, there’s a good mix of vocal workouts and instrumentals, with the man himself seemingly taking to the mic alongside a wealth of guests and collaborators. … Read more

 Never Sleeps Never Slumbers (Disco mix)
 Review: Quite how 'Pure Black Soul' spent 44 years gathering dust is anyone's guess, because West Coast gospel singer and bassist Harvell Guiton had clearly struck gold in that Oakland studio in 1982. Recorded before his scarce 1983 debut single 'My Dream', the session is a spirited collision of church-bred soul and boogie mischief. 'Never Sleeps Never Slumbers' gets a particularly cheeky disco workout, then the trio take on 'My Dream' has a dusty, head-nodding charm. We'd have been knocking on the studio door decades ago. … Read more

 Strictly Necessary
 These cookies are required to deliver the service or functionality you request. They enable core features such as page navigation, secure access, authentication, and load balancing. Without them, the website cannot function properly. Consent is not required for these cookies.
 Performance
 These cookies collect information about how visitors use our website, such as page visits and traffic sources. This data helps us measure performance and improve our site and services. These cookies are only used with your consent.
 Affiliate and Marketing
 These cookies are set by our advertising partners to track browsing activity and build profiles based on your interests. This allows personalised advertising and remarketing on other websites. These cookies are only used with your consent.
